This confirms it definitively — this ESP32 board and wiring is 100% good. BUSY toggles HIGH→LOW exactly as expected, begin() succeeds, and you get the same clean HW=0x22/DEV=0x3/FW=1.1 readback as your Teensy. So this proves both your LR1121 module and this ESP32 board/wiring are healthy.
What this tells us about the zero-board attempt
Since this exact code with pins NSS=5, IRQ=26, RST=14, BUSY=27, SCK=18, MISO=19, MOSI=23 works cleanly, and the zero-board version with RST=17, BUSY=16, IRQ=4 failed with BUSY stuck HIGH and -705 — the fault is isolated to something specific about the zero-board setup: either
A genuinely different physical module on that board (weaker unit), or
A wiring/solder issue specific to GPIO16, GPIO17, or GPIO4 on that particular ESP32, or
A power delivery problem specific to that physical board/breadboard setup (cold joint, bad rail, etc.)
Fastest way forward
Just use this exact known-good pin set for your real receiver build. No reason to keep chasing the zero-board pin assignment (5/18/23/19/17/16/4) when you have a byte-for-byte proven working configuration right here (5/18/23/19/14/27/26). Standardize on this one.
Full GCS-integrated receiver using this proven pin set
cpp
#include <RadioLib.h>
#define TEAM_ID "025"
#define PIN_NSS    5
#define PIN_IRQ   26
#define PIN_RST   14
#define PIN_BUSY  27
#define PIN_SCK   18
#define PIN_MISO  19
#define PIN_MOSI  23
#define FREQ_MAIN      867.00f
#define LORA_SF        10
#define LORA_BW        125.0f
#define LORA_CR        5
#define LORA_SYNC      0x98
#define LORA_PREAMBLE  12
#define TCXO_VOLTAGE   0.0f
#define PKT_MSG_LEN 10
#define PKT_MAGIC   0xA5
typedef struct __attribute__((packed)) {
  uint8_t  magic;
  uint8_t  teamId;
  uint32_t seq;
  uint32_t upMs;
  int8_t   txPower;
  uint8_t  paPath;
  uint8_t  flags;
  char     msg[PKT_MSG_LEN];
  uint8_t  crc8;
} RangePkt_t;
#define PKT_LEN ((uint8_t)sizeof(RangePkt_t))
static_assert(sizeof(RangePkt_t) == 24, "packet must be 24 bytes");
SPIClass loraSPI(HSPI);
LR1121 radio = new Module(PIN_NSS, PIN_IRQ, PIN_RST, PIN_BUSY, loraSPI);
static const uint32_t rfsw_pins[] = {
  RADIOLIB_LR11X0_DIO5, RADIOLIB_LR11X0_DIO6,
  RADIOLIB_NC, RADIOLIB_NC, RADIOLIB_NC
};
static const Module::RfSwitchMode_t rfsw_table[] = {
  { LR11x0::MODE_STBY,  { LOW,  LOW  } },
  { LR11x0::MODE_RX,    { HIGH, LOW  } },
  { LR11x0::MODE_TX,    { HIGH, HIGH } },
  { LR11x0::MODE_TX_HP, { LOW,  HIGH } },
  { LR11x0::MODE_TX_HF, { LOW,  LOW  } },
  { LR11x0::MODE_GNSS,  { LOW,  LOW  } },
  { LR11x0::MODE_WIFI,  { LOW,  LOW  } },
  END_OF_MODE_TABLE,
};
volatile bool pktFlag = false;
uint32_t packetCount = 0;
uint32_t startMs = 0;
IRAM_ATTR void onPacket() { pktFlag = true; }
static uint8_t crc8(const uint8_t* d, size_t n) {
  uint8_t c = 0;
  while (n--) {
    c ^= *d++;
    for (uint8_t i = 0; i < 8; i++)
      c = (c & 0x80) ? (uint8_t)((c << 1) ^ 0x07) : (uint8_t)(c << 1);
  }
  return c;
}
static bool rfCheck(const __FlashStringHelper* what, int16_t st) {
  if (st == RADIOLIB_ERR_NONE) return true;
  Serial.print(F("  !! ")); Serial.print(what);
  Serial.print(F(" -> code ")); Serial.println(st);
  return false;
}
#define RFC(n, c) rfCheck(F(n), (c))
void applyRxConfig() {
  RFC("setFrequency",       radio.setFrequency(FREQ_MAIN));
  RFC("setBandwidth",       radio.setBandwidth(LORA_BW));
  RFC("setSpreadingFactor", radio.setSpreadingFactor(LORA_SF));
  RFC("setCodingRate",      radio.setCodingRate(LORA_CR));
  RFC("setSyncWord",        radio.setSyncWord(LORA_SYNC));
  RFC("setPreambleLength",  radio.setPreambleLength(LORA_PREAMBLE));
  RFC("setCRC",             radio.setCRC(2));
  RFC("implicitHeader",     radio.implicitHeader(PKT_LEN));
  RFC("setRegulatorDCDC",   radio.setRegulatorDCDC());
  RFC("setRxBoostedGainMode", radio.setRxBoostedGainMode(true));
}
#define N_SCHEMA_FIELDS 79
#define IDX_TIME_S   0
#define IDX_PKT      1
#define IDX_RSSI     61
#define IDX_SNR      62
#define IDX_FREQ_MHZ 63
void sendGcsLine(float rssi, float snr, float freqMhz) {
  float t = (millis() - startMs) / 1000.0;
  Serial.print(TEAM_ID);
  Serial.print(",");
  for (int i = 0; i < N_SCHEMA_FIELDS; i++) {
    if (i == IDX_TIME_S)        Serial.print(t, 2);
    else if (i == IDX_PKT)      Serial.print(packetCount);
    else if (i == IDX_RSSI)     Serial.print(rssi, 1);
    else if (i == IDX_SNR)      Serial.print(snr, 1);
    else if (i == IDX_FREQ_MHZ) Serial.print(freqMhz, 2);
    if (i < N_SCHEMA_FIELDS - 1) Serial.print(",");
  }
  Serial.println();
}
void setup() {
  Serial.begin(115200);
  delay(600);
  Serial.println(F("=== VRITRA TEAM 025 -- ESP32 LoRa RECEIVER (GCS) ==="));
  loraSPI.begin(PIN_SCK, PIN_MISO, PIN_MOSI, PIN_NSS);
  radio.setRfSwitchTable(rfsw_pins, rfsw_table);
  Serial.print(F("[RF ] begin() ... "));
  int16_t st = radio.begin(FREQ_MAIN, LORA_BW, LORA_SF, LORA_CR,
                           LORA_SYNC, 10, LORA_PREAMBLE, TCXO_VOLTAGE);
  if (st != RADIOLIB_ERR_NONE) {
    Serial.print(F("FAILED ")); Serial.println(st);
    while (1) delay(500);
  }
  Serial.println(F("OK"));
  radio.setRfSwitchTable(rfsw_pins, rfsw_table);
  applyRxConfig();
  radio.setPacketReceivedAction(onPacket);
  radio.startReceive();
  startMs = millis();
  Serial.println(F("Listening. Point your GCS at this COM port, 115200 baud."));
}
void loop() {
  if (!pktFlag) return;
  pktFlag = false;
  RangePkt_t p;
  int16_t st = radio.readData((uint8_t*)&p, PKT_LEN);
  if (st != RADIOLIB_ERR_NONE) { radio.startReceive(); return; }
  if (p.magic != PKT_MAGIC)    { radio.startReceive(); return; }
  if (crc8((const uint8_t*)&p, sizeof(p) - 1) != p.crc8) { radio.startReceive(); return; }
  packetCount++;
  float rssi = radio.getRSSI();
  float snr  = radio.getSNR();
  sendGcsLine(rssi, snr, FREQ_MAIN);
  char msgbuf[PKT_MSG_LEN + 1];
  memcpy(msgbuf, p.msg, PKT_MSG_LEN);
  msgbuf[PKT_MSG_LEN] = '\0';
  Serial.print(F("  ^ seq=")); Serial.print(p.seq);
  Serial.print(F(" RSSI=")); Serial.print(rssi, 1);
  Serial.print(F(" SNR="));  Serial.print(snr, 1);
  Serial.print(F(" msg=\"")); Serial.print(msgbuf);
  Serial.println(F("\""));
  radio.startReceive();
}
Update your Teensy transmitter too
Same TCXO_VOLTAGE = 0.0f fix should already be in place from earlier — confirm it, since your barebone test used radio.begin() with defaults, which doesn't test the TCXO path. In your actual transmitter sketch, keep TCXO_VOLTAGE 0.0f as before.
Final wiring reference — use this everywhere from now on
Core1121 pin	ESP32 GPIO
NSS	5
SCK	18
MOSI	23
MISO	19
RESET	14
BUSY	27
IRQ/DIO9	26
3.3V	3.3V
GND	GND
Solder your permanent board to this pinout — it's proven, not theoretical. Flash this receiver + your working Teensy transmitter, and you should be back to full TX/RX communication.ive me teensy full code for transmission
Here's the complete Teensy 4.1 transmitter, matching your proven-working pin set and the TCXO fix:
cpp

```cpp
#include <RadioLib.h>

// ==== Pin mapping -- confirmed working (HW=0x22 DEV=0x3 FW=1.1) ====
#define PIN_NSS   10
#define PIN_IRQ   15    // DIO9 on Core1121-XF
#define PIN_RST   16
#define PIN_BUSY  14
// MOSI=11, MISO=12, SCK=13 are Teensy 4.1's fixed hardware SPI0 pins

#define FREQ_MAIN      867.00f
#define FREQ_BACKUP    865.75f
#define LORA_SF        10
#define LORA_BW        125.0f
#define LORA_CR        5
#define LORA_SYNC      0x98
#define LORA_PREAMBLE  12
#define TX_POWER_DBM   14        // +14 flight legal max, +10 on pad
#define TCXO_VOLTAGE   0.0f      // Core1121-XF uses plain crystal, not TCXO

#define PKT_MSG_LEN    10
#define PKT_MAGIC      0xA5

typedef struct __attribute__((packed)) {
  uint8_t  magic;
  uint8_t  teamId;
  uint32_t seq;
  uint32_t upMs;
  int8_t   txPower;
  uint8_t  paPath;
  uint8_t  flags;
  char     msg[PKT_MSG_LEN];
  uint8_t  crc8;
} RangePkt_t;
#define PKT_LEN ((uint8_t)sizeof(RangePkt_t))
static_assert(sizeof(RangePkt_t) == 24, "packet must be 24 bytes");

LR1121 radio = new Module(PIN_NSS, PIN_IRQ, PIN_RST, PIN_BUSY, SPI);

static const uint32_t rfsw_pins[] = {
  RADIOLIB_LR11X0_DIO5, RADIOLIB_LR11X0_DIO6,
  RADIOLIB_NC, RADIOLIB_NC, RADIOLIB_NC
};
static const Module::RfSwitchMode_t rfsw_table[] = {
  { LR11x0::MODE_STBY,  { LOW,  LOW  } },
  { LR11x0::MODE_RX,    { HIGH, LOW  } },
  { LR11x0::MODE_TX,    { HIGH, HIGH } },
  { LR11x0::MODE_TX_HP, { LOW,  HIGH } },   // matched high-power PA pin
  { LR11x0::MODE_TX_HF, { LOW,  LOW  } },
  { LR11x0::MODE_GNSS,  { LOW,  LOW  } },
  { LR11x0::MODE_WIFI,  { LOW,  LOW  } },
  END_OF_MODE_TABLE,
};

uint32_t seqNum = 0;
bool useBackup = false;
bool txEnabled = true;
String lineBuf = "";
String pendingMsg = "BEACON";
uint32_t lastBeacon = 0;
#define BEACON_MS 3000

static uint8_t crc8(const uint8_t* d, size_t n) {
  uint8_t c = 0;
  while (n--) {
    c ^= *d++;
    for (uint8_t i = 0; i < 8; i++)
      c = (c & 0x80) ? (uint8_t)((c << 1) ^ 0x07) : (uint8_t)(c << 1);
  }
  return c;
}

static bool rfCheck(const __FlashStringHelper* what, int16_t st) {
  if (st == RADIOLIB_ERR_NONE) return true;
  Serial.print(F("  !! ")); Serial.print(what);
  Serial.print(F(" -> code ")); Serial.println(st);
  return false;
}
#define RFC(n, c) rfCheck(F(n), (c))

void applyRadioConfig() {
  float f = useBackup ? FREQ_BACKUP : FREQ_MAIN;
  RFC("setFrequency",       radio.setFrequency(f));
  RFC("setBandwidth",       radio.setBandwidth(LORA_BW));
  RFC("setSpreadingFactor", radio.setSpreadingFactor(LORA_SF));
  RFC("setCodingRate",      radio.setCodingRate(LORA_CR));
  RFC("setSyncWord",        radio.setSyncWord(LORA_SYNC));
  RFC("setPreambleLength",  radio.setPreambleLength(LORA_PREAMBLE));
  RFC("setCRC",             radio.setCRC(2));
  RFC("implicitHeader",     radio.implicitHeader(PKT_LEN));
  RFC("setRegulatorDCDC",   radio.setRegulatorDCDC());
  RFC("setOutputPower(HP)", radio.setOutputPower(TX_POWER_DBM, true));

  Serial.print(F("[RF ] ")); Serial.print(f, 2);
  Serial.print(F(" MHz SF")); Serial.print(LORA_SF);
  Serial.print(F(" BW")); Serial.print(LORA_BW, 0);
  Serial.print(F("k CR4/")); Serial.print(LORA_CR);
  Serial.print(F(" Sync 0x")); Serial.print(LORA_SYNC, HEX);
  Serial.print(F(" ")); Serial.print(TX_POWER_DBM);
  Serial.println(F(" dBm HP-PA"));
}

void setup() {
  Serial.begin(115200);
  while (!Serial && millis() < 4000) {}
  delay(500);

  Serial.println();
  Serial.println(F("======================================================"));
  Serial.println(F("  VRITRA TEAM 025 -- Teensy 4.1 LoRa TRANSMITTER"));
  Serial.println(F("======================================================"));
  Serial.print(F("Pins: NSS=")); Serial.print(PIN_NSS);
  Serial.print(F(" IRQ=")); Serial.print(PIN_IRQ);
  Serial.print(F(" RST=")); Serial.print(PIN_RST);
  Serial.print(F(" BUSY=")); Serial.println(PIN_BUSY);

  SPI.begin();
  radio.setRfSwitchTable(rfsw_pins, rfsw_table);

  Serial.print(F("[RF ] begin() ... "));
  int16_t st = radio.begin(FREQ_MAIN, LORA_BW, LORA_SF, LORA_CR,
                           LORA_SYNC, TX_POWER_DBM, LORA_PREAMBLE, TCXO_VOLTAGE);
  if (st != RADIOLIB_ERR_NONE) {
    Serial.print(F("FAILED ")); Serial.println(st);
    while (1) delay(500);
  }
  Serial.println(F("OK"));
  radio.setRfSwitchTable(rfsw_pins, rfsw_table);

  LR11x0VersionInfo_t vi;
  if (radio.getVersionInfo(&vi) == RADIOLIB_ERR_NONE) {
    Serial.print(F("[RF ] HW 0x")); Serial.print(vi.hardware, HEX);
    Serial.print(F(" DEV 0x"));     Serial.print(vi.device, HEX);
    Serial.print(F(" FW "));        Serial.print(vi.fwMajor);
    Serial.print(F("."));           Serial.println(vi.fwMinor);
  }

  applyRadioConfig();

  Serial.print(F("[RF ] airtime for "));
  Serial.print(PKT_LEN);
  Serial.print(F(" B: "));
  Serial.print(radio.getTimeOnAir(PKT_LEN) / 1000.0, 1);
  Serial.println(F(" ms"));

  Serial.println();
  Serial.println(F("Commands (type + Enter):"));
  Serial.println(F("  <text>       send as payload (max 10 ch), also becomes new beacon msg"));
  Serial.println(F("  /freq main   867.00 MHz"));
  Serial.println(F("  /freq back   865.75 MHz"));
  Serial.println(F("  /tx off      telecommand silence"));
  Serial.println(F("  /tx on       re-enable"));
  Serial.println(F("  /status      print current config"));
  Serial.println(F("======================================================"));
  lastBeacon = millis();
}

void sendPacket(const String& text) {
  if (!txEnabled) {
    Serial.println(F("[TX ] blocked, transmitter OFF"));
    return;
  }
  seqNum++;

  RangePkt_t p;
  memset(&p, 0, sizeof(p));
  p.magic   = PKT_MAGIC;
  p.teamId  = 25;
  p.seq     = seqNum;
  p.upMs    = millis();
  p.txPower = TX_POWER_DBM;
  p.paPath  = 'H';
  p.flags   = useBackup ? 0x01 : 0x00;
  memset(p.msg, ' ', PKT_MSG_LEN);
  for (uint8_t i = 0; i < PKT_MSG_LEN && i < text.length(); i++) p.msg[i] = text[i];
  p.crc8 = crc8((const uint8_t*)&p, sizeof(p) - 1);

  uint32_t t0 = millis();
  int16_t st = radio.transmit((uint8_t*)&p, sizeof(p));
  uint32_t dt = millis() - t0;

  Serial.print(F("[TX ] seq=")); Serial.print(seqNum);
  Serial.print(F(" ")); Serial.print(dt); Serial.print(F("ms \""));
  Serial.print(text); Serial.print(F("\" "));
  Serial.println(st == RADIOLIB_ERR_NONE ? F("OK") : F("FAILED"));
}

void handleLine(String s) {
  s.trim();
  if (!s.length()) return;

  if (s.equalsIgnoreCase("/tx off")) {
    txEnabled = false;
    radio.standby();
    Serial.println(F("[CMD] TX OFF"));
  } else if (s.equalsIgnoreCase("/tx on")) {
    txEnabled = true;
    applyRadioConfig();
    Serial.println(F("[CMD] TX ON"));
  } else if (s.equalsIgnoreCase("/freq main")) {
    useBackup = false;
    applyRadioConfig();
  } else if (s.equalsIgnoreCase("/freq back")) {
    useBackup = true;
    applyRadioConfig();
  } else if (s.equalsIgnoreCase("/status")) {
    Serial.print(F("[ST ] freq=")); Serial.print(useBackup ? FREQ_BACKUP : FREQ_MAIN, 2);
    Serial.print(F(" tx="));        Serial.print(txEnabled ? F("ON") : F("OFF"));
    Serial.print(F(" sent="));      Serial.println(seqNum);
  } else {
    pendingMsg = s.substring(0, PKT_MSG_LEN);
    sendPacket(pendingMsg);
  }
}

void feedChar(char c) {
  if (c == '\n' || c == '\r') {
    if (lineBuf.length()) { handleLine(lineBuf); lineBuf = ""; }
  } else {
    lineBuf += c;
    if (lineBuf.length() > 200) lineBuf = "";
  }
}

void loop() {
  while (Serial.available()) feedChar((char)Serial.read());

  if (millis() - lastBeacon >= BEACON_MS) {
    lastBeacon = millis();
    sendPacket(pendingMsg);
  }
}
```

This matches the exact pin set that gave you the clean `HW=0x22 DEV=0x3 FW=1.1` readback, plus:

* `TCXO_VOLTAGE = 0.0f` fix
* HP PA forced via `setOutputPower(TX_POWER_DBM, true)`
* Implicit header (rejects corrupted noise at low SNR)
* Auto-beacon every 3s, or type text to send on demand
* `/freq main` and `/freq back` for manual frequency switching (main/backup)
* `/tx off` / `/tx on` for telecommand silence testing

Found it — and it's a real ordering bug in my code, not your wiring. Compare the two:
Barebone test (worked): called `radio.begin()` with defaults, no `setRfSwitchTable()` call before it.
Full code (fails -707): calls `radio.setRfSwitchTable(...)` before `radio.begin()`.
That's the difference. `setRfSwitchTable()` sends an SPI command configuring the chip's DIO pin mapping — but the chip isn't fully initialized/reset yet at that point in my code's sequence, so that command either gets rejected or leaves the chip's SPI interface in a bad state, causing the subsequent `begin()` call to time out.
Fix — move the switch table call to AFTER begin() succeeds
In both sketches (Teensy and ESP32), find this order in `setup()`:
cpp

```cpp
loraSPI.begin(...);          // or SPI.begin() on Teensy
radio.setRfSwitchTable(rfsw_pins, rfsw_table);   // <-- WRONG, too early

Serial.print(F("[RF ] begin() ... "));
int16_t st = radio.begin(...);
```

Change it to:
cpp

```cpp
loraSPI.begin(...);          // or SPI.begin() on Teensy

Serial.print(F("[RF ] begin() ... "));
int16_t st = radio.begin(...);
if (st != RADIOLIB_ERR_NONE) {
  Serial.print(F("FAILED ")); Serial.println(st);
  while (1) delay(500);
}
Serial.println(F("OK"));

radio.setRfSwitchTable(rfsw_pins, rfsw_table);   // <-- moved here, after begin()
```

What to do
In both your Teensy transmitter and ESP32 receiver sketches, delete the first `radio.setRfSwitchTable(...)` call (the one before `begin()`), and keep only the second one (the one that already exists right after the `Serial.println(F("OK"));` line, right after the begin() success check). Both sketches actually already have that second call in place — the fix is just removing the redundant early one that's causing the failure.
Reflash both, and this should get you back to the exact clean `begin() OK` / `HW=0x22 DEV=0x3 FW=1.1` result you had in the barebone test.

Here are both full sketches with the fix applied — `setRfSwitchTable()` now only called once, after `begin()` succeeds.
Teensy 4.1 — Transmitter
cpp

```cpp
#include <RadioLib.h>

// ==== Pin mapping -- confirmed working (HW=0x22 DEV=0x3 FW=1.1) ====
#define PIN_NSS   10
#define PIN_IRQ   15    // DIO9 on Core1121-XF
#define PIN_RST   16
#define PIN_BUSY  14
// MOSI=11, MISO=12, SCK=13 are Teensy 4.1's fixed hardware SPI0 pins

#define FREQ_MAIN      867.00f
#define FREQ_BACKUP    865.75f
#define LORA_SF        10
#define LORA_BW        125.0f
#define LORA_CR        5
#define LORA_SYNC      0x98
#define LORA_PREAMBLE  12
#define TX_POWER_DBM   14
#define TCXO_VOLTAGE   0.0f

#define PKT_MSG_LEN    10
#define PKT_MAGIC      0xA5

typedef struct __attribute__((packed)) {
  uint8_t  magic;
  uint8_t  teamId;
  uint32_t seq;
  uint32_t upMs;
  int8_t   txPower;
  uint8_t  paPath;
  uint8_t  flags;
  char     msg[PKT_MSG_LEN];
  uint8_t  crc8;
} RangePkt_t;
#define PKT_LEN ((uint8_t)sizeof(RangePkt_t))
static_assert(sizeof(RangePkt_t) == 24, "packet must be 24 bytes");

LR1121 radio = new Module(PIN_NSS, PIN_IRQ, PIN_RST, PIN_BUSY, SPI);

static const uint32_t rfsw_pins[] = {
  RADIOLIB_LR11X0_DIO5, RADIOLIB_LR11X0_DIO6,
  RADIOLIB_NC, RADIOLIB_NC, RADIOLIB_NC
};
static const Module::RfSwitchMode_t rfsw_table[] = {
  { LR11x0::MODE_STBY,  { LOW,  LOW  } },
  { LR11x0::MODE_RX,    { HIGH, LOW  } },
  { LR11x0::MODE_TX,    { HIGH, HIGH } },
  { LR11x0::MODE_TX_HP, { LOW,  HIGH } },
  { LR11x0::MODE_TX_HF, { LOW,  LOW  } },
  { LR11x0::MODE_GNSS,  { LOW,  LOW  } },
  { LR11x0::MODE_WIFI,  { LOW,  LOW  } },
  END_OF_MODE_TABLE,
};

uint32_t seqNum = 0;
bool useBackup = false;
bool txEnabled = true;
String lineBuf = "";
String pendingMsg = "BEACON";
uint32_t lastBeacon = 0;
#define BEACON_MS 3000

static uint8_t crc8(const uint8_t* d, size_t n) {
  uint8_t c = 0;
  while (n--) {
    c ^= *d++;
    for (uint8_t i = 0; i < 8; i++)
      c = (c & 0x80) ? (uint8_t)((c << 1) ^ 0x07) : (uint8_t)(c << 1);
  }
  return c;
}

static bool rfCheck(const __FlashStringHelper* what, int16_t st) {
  if (st == RADIOLIB_ERR_NONE) return true;
  Serial.print(F("  !! ")); Serial.print(what);
  Serial.print(F(" -> code ")); Serial.println(st);
  return false;
}
#define RFC(n, c) rfCheck(F(n), (c))

void applyRadioConfig() {
  float f = useBackup ? FREQ_BACKUP : FREQ_MAIN;
  RFC("setFrequency",       radio.setFrequency(f));
  RFC("setBandwidth",       radio.setBandwidth(LORA_BW));
  RFC("setSpreadingFactor", radio.setSpreadingFactor(LORA_SF));
  RFC("setCodingRate",      radio.setCodingRate(LORA_CR));
  RFC("setSyncWord",        radio.setSyncWord(LORA_SYNC));
  RFC("setPreambleLength",  radio.setPreambleLength(LORA_PREAMBLE));
  RFC("setCRC",             radio.setCRC(2));
  RFC("implicitHeader",     radio.implicitHeader(PKT_LEN));
  RFC("setRegulatorDCDC",   radio.setRegulatorDCDC());
  RFC("setOutputPower(HP)", radio.setOutputPower(TX_POWER_DBM, true));

  Serial.print(F("[RF ] ")); Serial.print(f, 2);
  Serial.print(F(" MHz SF")); Serial.print(LORA_SF);
  Serial.print(F(" BW")); Serial.print(LORA_BW, 0);
  Serial.print(F("k CR4/")); Serial.print(LORA_CR);
  Serial.print(F(" Sync 0x")); Serial.print(LORA_SYNC, HEX);
  Serial.print(F(" ")); Serial.print(TX_POWER_DBM);
  Serial.println(F(" dBm HP-PA"));
}

void setup() {
  Serial.begin(115200);
  while (!Serial && millis() < 4000) {}
  delay(500);

  Serial.println();
  Serial.println(F("======================================================"));
  Serial.println(F("  VRITRA TEAM 025 -- Teensy 4.1 LoRa TRANSMITTER"));
  Serial.println(F("======================================================"));
  Serial.print(F("Pins: NSS=")); Serial.print(PIN_NSS);
  Serial.print(F(" IRQ=")); Serial.print(PIN_IRQ);
  Serial.print(F(" RST=")); Serial.print(PIN_RST);
  Serial.print(F(" BUSY=")); Serial.println(PIN_BUSY);

  // ---- SPI init only. NO setRfSwitchTable before begin(). ----
  SPI.begin();

  Serial.print(F("[RF ] begin() ... "));
  int16_t st = radio.begin(FREQ_MAIN, LORA_BW, LORA_SF, LORA_CR,
                           LORA_SYNC, TX_POWER_DBM, LORA_PREAMBLE, TCXO_VOLTAGE);
  if (st != RADIOLIB_ERR_NONE) {
    Serial.print(F("FAILED ")); Serial.println(st);
    while (1) delay(500);
  }
  Serial.println(F("OK"));

  // ---- setRfSwitchTable ONLY after begin() succeeds ----
  radio.setRfSwitchTable(rfsw_pins, rfsw_table);

  LR11x0VersionInfo_t vi;
  if (radio.getVersionInfo(&vi) == RADIOLIB_ERR_NONE) {
    Serial.print(F("[RF ] HW 0x")); Serial.print(vi.hardware, HEX);
    Serial.print(F(" DEV 0x"));     Serial.print(vi.device, HEX);
    Serial.print(F(" FW "));        Serial.print(vi.fwMajor);
    Serial.print(F("."));           Serial.println(vi.fwMinor);
  }

  applyRadioConfig();

  Serial.print(F("[RF ] airtime for "));
  Serial.print(PKT_LEN);
  Serial.print(F(" B: "));
  Serial.print(radio.getTimeOnAir(PKT_LEN) / 1000.0, 1);
  Serial.println(F(" ms"));

  Serial.println();
  Serial.println(F("Commands (type + Enter):"));
  Serial.println(F("  <text>       send as payload (max 10 ch), becomes new beacon msg"));
  Serial.println(F("  /freq main   867.00 MHz"));
  Serial.println(F("  /freq back   865.75 MHz"));
  Serial.println(F("  /tx off      telecommand silence"));
  Serial.println(F("  /tx on       re-enable"));
  Serial.println(F("  /status      print current config"));
  Serial.println(F("======================================================"));
  lastBeacon = millis();
}

void sendPacket(const String& text) {
  if (!txEnabled) {
    Serial.println(F("[TX ] blocked, transmitter OFF"));
    return;
  }
  seqNum++;

  RangePkt_t p;
  memset(&p, 0, sizeof(p));
  p.magic   = PKT_MAGIC;
  p.teamId  = 25;
  p.seq     = seqNum;
  p.upMs    = millis();
  p.txPower = TX_POWER_DBM;
  p.paPath  = 'H';
  p.flags   = useBackup ? 0x01 : 0x00;
  memset(p.msg, ' ', PKT_MSG_LEN);
  for (uint8_t i = 0; i < PKT_MSG_LEN && i < text.length(); i++) p.msg[i] = text[i];
  p.crc8 = crc8((const uint8_t*)&p, sizeof(p) - 1);

  uint32_t t0 = millis();
  int16_t st = radio.transmit((uint8_t*)&p, sizeof(p));
  uint32_t dt = millis() - t0;

  Serial.print(F("[TX ] seq=")); Serial.print(seqNum);
  Serial.print(F(" ")); Serial.print(dt); Serial.print(F("ms \""));
  Serial.print(text); Serial.print(F("\" "));
  Serial.println(st == RADIOLIB_ERR_NONE ? F("OK") : F("FAILED"));
}

void handleLine(String s) {
  s.trim();
  if (!s.length()) return;

  if (s.equalsIgnoreCase("/tx off")) {
    txEnabled = false;
    radio.standby();
    Serial.println(F("[CMD] TX OFF"));
  } else if (s.equalsIgnoreCase("/tx on")) {
    txEnabled = true;
    applyRadioConfig();
    Serial.println(F("[CMD] TX ON"));
  } else if (s.equalsIgnoreCase("/freq main")) {
    useBackup = false;
    applyRadioConfig();
  } else if (s.equalsIgnoreCase("/freq back")) {
    useBackup = true;
    applyRadioConfig();
  } else if (s.equalsIgnoreCase("/status")) {
    Serial.print(F("[ST ] freq=")); Serial.print(useBackup ? FREQ_BACKUP : FREQ_MAIN, 2);
    Serial.print(F(" tx="));        Serial.print(txEnabled ? F("ON") : F("OFF"));
    Serial.print(F(" sent="));      Serial.println(seqNum);
  } else {
    pendingMsg = s.substring(0, PKT_MSG_LEN);
    sendPacket(pendingMsg);
  }
}

void feedChar(char c) {
  if (c == '\n' || c == '\r') {
    if (lineBuf.length()) { handleLine(lineBuf); lineBuf = ""; }
  } else {
    lineBuf += c;
    if (lineBuf.length() > 200) lineBuf = "";
  }
}

void loop() {
  while (Serial.available()) feedChar((char)Serial.read());

  if (millis() - lastBeacon >= BEACON_MS) {
    lastBeacon = millis();
    sendPacket(pendingMsg);
  }
}
```

ESP32 — Receiver (GCS-integrated)
cpp

```cpp
#include <RadioLib.h>

#define TEAM_ID "025"

#define PIN_NSS    5
#define PIN_IRQ   26
#define PIN_RST   14
#define PIN_BUSY  27
#define PIN_SCK   18
#define PIN_MISO  19
#define PIN_MOSI  23

#define FREQ_MAIN      867.00f
#define LORA_SF        10
#define LORA_BW        125.0f
#define LORA_CR        5
#define LORA_SYNC      0x98
#define LORA_PREAMBLE  12
#define TCXO_VOLTAGE   0.0f

#define PKT_MSG_LEN 10
#define PKT_MAGIC   0xA5

typedef struct __attribute__((packed)) {
  uint8_t  magic;
  uint8_t  teamId;
  uint32_t seq;
  uint32_t upMs;
  int8_t   txPower;
  uint8_t  paPath;
  uint8_t  flags;
  char     msg[PKT_MSG_LEN];
  uint8_t  crc8;
} RangePkt_t;
#define PKT_LEN ((uint8_t)sizeof(RangePkt_t))
static_assert(sizeof(RangePkt_t) == 24, "packet must be 24 bytes");

SPIClass loraSPI(HSPI);
LR1121 radio = new Module(PIN_NSS, PIN_IRQ, PIN_RST, PIN_BUSY, loraSPI);

static const uint32_t rfsw_pins[] = {
  RADIOLIB_LR11X0_DIO5, RADIOLIB_LR11X0_DIO6,
  RADIOLIB_NC, RADIOLIB_NC, RADIOLIB_NC
};
static const Module::RfSwitchMode_t rfsw_table[] = {
  { LR11x0::MODE_STBY,  { LOW,  LOW  } },
  { LR11x0::MODE_RX,    { HIGH, LOW  } },
  { LR11x0::MODE_TX,    { HIGH, HIGH } },
  { LR11x0::MODE_TX_HP, { LOW,  HIGH } },
  { LR11x0::MODE_TX_HF, { LOW,  LOW  } },
  { LR11x0::MODE_GNSS,  { LOW,  LOW  } },
  { LR11x0::MODE_WIFI,  { LOW,  LOW  } },
  END_OF_MODE_TABLE,
};

volatile bool pktFlag = false;
uint32_t packetCount = 0;
uint32_t startMs = 0;

IRAM_ATTR void onPacket() { pktFlag = true; }

static uint8_t crc8(const uint8_t* d, size_t n) {
  uint8_t c = 0;
  while (n--) {
    c ^= *d++;
    for (uint8_t i = 0; i < 8; i++)
      c = (c & 0x80) ? (uint8_t)((c << 1) ^ 0x07) : (uint8_t)(c << 1);
  }
  return c;
}

static bool rfCheck(const __FlashStringHelper* what, int16_t st) {
  if (st == RADIOLIB_ERR_NONE) return true;
  Serial.print(F("  !! ")); Serial.print(what);
  Serial.print(F(" -> code ")); Serial.println(st);
  return false;
}
#define RFC(n, c) rfCheck(F(n), (c))

void applyRxConfig() {
  RFC("setFrequency",       radio.setFrequency(FREQ_MAIN));
  RFC("setBandwidth",       radio.setBandwidth(LORA_BW));
  RFC("setSpreadingFactor", radio.setSpreadingFactor(LORA_SF));
  RFC("setCodingRate",      radio.setCodingRate(LORA_CR));
  RFC("setSyncWord",        radio.setSyncWord(LORA_SYNC));
  RFC("setPreambleLength",  radio.setPreambleLength(LORA_PREAMBLE));
  RFC("setCRC",             radio.setCRC(2));
  RFC("implicitHeader",     radio.implicitHeader(PKT_LEN));
  RFC("setRegulatorDCDC",   radio.setRegulatorDCDC());
  RFC("setRxBoostedGainMode", radio.setRxBoostedGainMode(true));
}

#define N_SCHEMA_FIELDS 79
#define IDX_TIME_S   0
#define IDX_PKT      1
#define IDX_RSSI     61
#define IDX_SNR      62
#define IDX_FREQ_MHZ 63

void sendGcsLine(float rssi, float snr, float freqMhz) {
  float t = (millis() - startMs) / 1000.0;
  Serial.print(TEAM_ID);
  Serial.print(",");
  for (int i = 0; i < N_SCHEMA_FIELDS; i++) {
    if (i == IDX_TIME_S)        Serial.print(t, 2);
    else if (i == IDX_PKT)      Serial.print(packetCount);
    else if (i == IDX_RSSI)     Serial.print(rssi, 1);
    else if (i == IDX_SNR)      Serial.print(snr, 1);
    else if (i == IDX_FREQ_MHZ) Serial.print(freqMhz, 2);
    if (i < N_SCHEMA_FIELDS - 1) Serial.print(",");
  }
  Serial.println();
}

void setup() {
  Serial.begin(115200);
  delay(600);
  Serial.println();
  Serial.println(F("======================================================"));
  Serial.println(F("  VRITRA TEAM 025 -- ESP32 LoRa RECEIVER (GCS)"));
  Serial.println(F("======================================================"));
  Serial.print(F("Pins: NSS=")); Serial.print(PIN_NSS);
  Serial.print(F(" IRQ=")); Serial.print(PIN_IRQ);
  Serial.print(F(" RST=")); Serial.print(PIN_RST);
  Serial.print(F(" BUSY=")); Serial.println(PIN_BUSY);

  // ---- SPI init only. NO setRfSwitchTable before begin(). ----
  loraSPI.begin(PIN_SCK, PIN_MISO, PIN_MOSI, PIN_NSS);

  Serial.print(F("[RF ] begin() ... "));
  int16_t st = radio.begin(FREQ_MAIN, LORA_BW, LORA_SF, LORA_CR,
                           LORA_SYNC, 10, LORA_PREAMBLE, TCXO_VOLTAGE);
  if (st != RADIOLIB_ERR_NONE) {
    Serial.print(F("FAILED ")); Serial.println(st);
    while (1) delay(500);
  }
  Serial.println(F("OK"));

  // ---- setRfSwitchTable ONLY after begin() succeeds ----
  radio.setRfSwitchTable(rfsw_pins, rfsw_table);

  LR11x0VersionInfo_t vi;
  if (radio.getVersionInfo(&vi) == RADIOLIB_ERR_NONE) {
    Serial.print(F("[RF ] HW 0x")); Serial.print(vi.hardware, HEX);
    Serial.print(F(" DEV 0x"));     Serial.print(vi.device, HEX);
    Serial.print(F(" FW "));        Serial.print(vi.fwMajor);
    Serial.print(F("."));           Serial.println(vi.fwMinor);
  }

  applyRxConfig();
  radio.setPacketReceivedAction(onPacket);
  radio.startReceive();
  startMs = millis();

  Serial.println(F("Listening. Point your GCS at this COM port, 115200 baud."));
  Serial.println(F("======================================================"));
}

void loop() {
  if (!pktFlag) return;
  pktFlag = false;

  RangePkt_t p;
  int16_t st = radio.readData((uint8_t*)&p, PKT_LEN);
  if (st != RADIOLIB_ERR_NONE) { radio.startReceive(); return; }
  if (p.magic != PKT_MAGIC)    { radio.startReceive(); return; }
  if (crc8((const uint8_t*)&p, sizeof(p) - 1) != p.crc8) { radio.startReceive(); return; }

  packetCount++;
  float rssi = radio.getRSSI();
  float snr  = radio.getSNR();

  sendGcsLine(rssi, snr, FREQ_MAIN);

  char msgbuf[PKT_MSG_LEN + 1];
  memcpy(msgbuf, p.msg, PKT_MSG_LEN);
  msgbuf[PKT_MSG_LEN] = '\0';
  Serial.print(F("  ^ seq=")); Serial.print(p.seq);
  Serial.print(F(" RSSI=")); Serial.print(rssi, 1);
  Serial.print(F(" SNR="));  Serial.print(snr, 1);
  Serial.print(F(" msg=\"")); Serial.print(msgbuf);
  Serial.println(F("\""));

  radio.startReceive();
}
```

You're right to push back, and thank you for uploading this — it's the ground truth I needed. Let me be straight with you: I was wrong about the TCXO setting. I diagnosed -707 as "TCXO wrong, use 0.0f" based on reading your module's schematic and seeing a plain crystal, and concluded no TCXO existed. That was a bad inference. Your uploaded file proves empirically that `TCXO_VOLTAGE = 1.6f` is what actually works on this hardware, not 0.0f. I let a theoretical schematic reading override what should have been settled by testing.
I also need to correct my other claim — I said moving `setRfSwitchTable()` to after `begin()` was "the fix." Your working file calls it both before and after `begin()`, and it works fine. So that reordering theory was also wrong; it wasn't the actual cause of your earlier failures.
What actually was probably going on
Looking back at the failure pattern, the real variable that changed between your failing attempts and this working file is the TCXO value — 1.6f, not 0.0f. Everything else (pins, switch table order, packet structure) was already fine on this ESP32/module pairing.
Rebuilding both sketches from this proven baseline
I'm keeping your working file's exact structure — same call order, same TCXO=1.6f — and only changing pins for the Teensy side, plus adding GCS CSV output to the receiver.
Teensy 4.1 — Transmitter (rebuilt from your proven pattern)
cpp

```cpp
#include <RadioLib.h>

#define PIN_NSS   10
#define PIN_IRQ   15
#define PIN_RST   16
#define PIN_BUSY  14
// MOSI=11, MISO=12, SCK=13 are Teensy 4.1's fixed hardware SPI0 pins

#define FREQ_MAIN      867.00f
#define FREQ_BACKUP    865.75f
#define LORA_SF        10
#define LORA_BW        125.0f
#define LORA_CR        5
#define LORA_SYNC      0x98
#define LORA_PREAMBLE  12
#define TX_POWER_DBM   14
#define TCXO_VOLTAGE   1.6f     // proven working value on this hardware

#define PKT_MSG_LEN    10
#define PKT_MAGIC      0xA5

typedef struct __attribute__((packed)) {
  uint8_t  magic;
  uint8_t  teamId;
  uint32_t seq;
  uint32_t upMs;
  int8_t   txPower;
  uint8_t  paPath;
  uint8_t  flags;
  char     msg[PKT_MSG_LEN];
  uint8_t  crc8;
} RangePkt_t;
#define PKT_LEN ((uint8_t)sizeof(RangePkt_t))
static_assert(sizeof(RangePkt_t) == 24, "packet must be 24 bytes");

LR1121 radio = new Module(PIN_NSS, PIN_IRQ, PIN_RST, PIN_BUSY, SPI);

static const uint32_t rfsw_pins[] = {
  RADIOLIB_LR11X0_DIO5, RADIOLIB_LR11X0_DIO6,
  RADIOLIB_NC, RADIOLIB_NC, RADIOLIB_NC
};
static const Module::RfSwitchMode_t rfsw_table[] = {
  { LR11x0::MODE_STBY,  { LOW,  LOW  } },
  { LR11x0::MODE_RX,    { HIGH, LOW  } },
  { LR11x0::MODE_TX,    { HIGH, HIGH } },
  { LR11x0::MODE_TX_HP, { LOW,  HIGH } },
  { LR11x0::MODE_TX_HF, { LOW,  LOW  } },
  { LR11x0::MODE_GNSS,  { LOW,  LOW  } },
  { LR11x0::MODE_WIFI,  { LOW,  LOW  } },
  END_OF_MODE_TABLE,
};

uint32_t seqNum = 0;
bool useBackup = false;
bool txEnabled = true;
String lineBuf = "";
String pendingMsg = "BEACON";
uint32_t lastBeacon = 0;
#define BEACON_MS 3000

static uint8_t crc8(const uint8_t* d, size_t n) {
  uint8_t c = 0;
  while (n--) {
    c ^= *d++;
    for (uint8_t i = 0; i < 8; i++)
      c = (c & 0x80) ? (uint8_t)((c << 1) ^ 0x07) : (uint8_t)(c << 1);
  }
  return c;
}

static bool rfCheck(const __FlashStringHelper* what, int16_t st) {
  if (st == RADIOLIB_ERR_NONE) return true;
  Serial.print(F("  !! ")); Serial.print(what);
  Serial.print(F(" -> code ")); Serial.println(st);
  return false;
}
#define RFC(n, c) rfCheck(F(n), (c))

void applyRadioConfig() {
  float f = useBackup ? FREQ_BACKUP : FREQ_MAIN;
  RFC("setFrequency",       radio.setFrequency(f));
  RFC("setBandwidth",       radio.setBandwidth(LORA_BW));
  RFC("setSpreadingFactor", radio.setSpreadingFactor(LORA_SF));
  RFC("setCodingRate",      radio.setCodingRate(LORA_CR));
  RFC("setSyncWord",        radio.setSyncWord(LORA_SYNC));
  RFC("setPreambleLength",  radio.setPreambleLength(LORA_PREAMBLE));
  RFC("setCRC",             radio.setCRC(2));
  RFC("implicitHeader",     radio.implicitHeader(PKT_LEN));
  RFC("setRegulatorDCDC",   radio.setRegulatorDCDC());
  RFC("setOutputPower(HP)", radio.setOutputPower(TX_POWER_DBM, true));

  Serial.print(F("[RF ] ")); Serial.print(f, 2);
  Serial.print(F(" MHz SF")); Serial.print(LORA_SF);
  Serial.print(F(" BW")); Serial.print(LORA_BW, 0);
  Serial.print(F("k CR4/")); Serial.print(LORA_CR);
  Serial.print(F(" Sync 0x")); Serial.print(LORA_SYNC, HEX);
  Serial.print(F(" ")); Serial.print(TX_POWER_DBM);
  Serial.println(F(" dBm HP-PA"));
}

void setup() {
  Serial.begin(115200);
  while (!Serial && millis() < 4000) {}
  delay(500);
  Serial.println();
  Serial.println(F("======================================================"));
  Serial.println(F("  VRITRA TEAM 025 -- Teensy 4.1 LoRa TRANSMITTER"));
  Serial.println(F("======================================================"));
  Serial.print(F("Pins: NSS=")); Serial.print(PIN_NSS);
  Serial.print(F(" IRQ=")); Serial.print(PIN_IRQ);
  Serial.print(F(" RST=")); Serial.print(PIN_RST);
  Serial.print(F(" BUSY=")); Serial.println(PIN_BUSY);

  SPI.begin();
  radio.setRfSwitchTable(rfsw_pins, rfsw_table);   // before begin() -- matches proven file

  Serial.print(F("[RF ] begin() ... "));
  int16_t st = radio.begin(FREQ_MAIN, LORA_BW, LORA_SF, LORA_CR,
                           LORA_SYNC, TX_POWER_DBM, LORA_PREAMBLE, TCXO_VOLTAGE);
  if (st != RADIOLIB_ERR_NONE) {
    Serial.print(F("FAILED ")); Serial.println(st);
    while (1) delay(500);
  }
  Serial.println(F("OK"));
  radio.setRfSwitchTable(rfsw_pins, rfsw_table);   // after begin() too -- matches proven file

  LR11x0VersionInfo_t vi;
  if (radio.getVersionInfo(&vi) == RADIOLIB_ERR_NONE) {
    Serial.print(F("[RF ] HW 0x")); Serial.print(vi.hardware, HEX);
    Serial.print(F(" dev 0x"));     Serial.print(vi.device, HEX);
    Serial.print(F(" fw "));        Serial.print(vi.fwMajor);
    Serial.print(F("."));           Serial.println(vi.fwMinor);
  }

  applyRadioConfig();

  Serial.print(F("[RF ] airtime for "));
  Serial.print(PKT_LEN);
  Serial.print(F(" B: "));
  Serial.print(radio.getTimeOnAir(PKT_LEN) / 1000.0, 1);
  Serial.println(F(" ms"));

  Serial.println();
  Serial.println(F("Commands (type + Enter):"));
  Serial.println(F("  <text>       send as payload (max 10 ch), becomes new beacon msg"));
  Serial.println(F("  /freq main   867.00 MHz"));
  Serial.println(F("  /freq back   865.75 MHz"));
  Serial.println(F("  /tx off      telecommand silence"));
  Serial.println(F("  /tx on       re-enable"));
  Serial.println(F("  /status      print current config"));
  Serial.println(F("======================================================"));
  lastBeacon = millis();
}

void sendPacket(const String& text) {
  if (!txEnabled) {
    Serial.println(F("[TX ] blocked, transmitter OFF"));
    return;
  }
  seqNum++;

  RangePkt_t p;
  memset(&p, 0, sizeof(p));
  p.magic   = PKT_MAGIC;
  p.teamId  = 25;
  p.seq     = seqNum;
  p.upMs    = millis();
  p.txPower = TX_POWER_DBM;
  p.paPath  = 'H';
  p.flags   = useBackup ? 0x01 : 0x00;
  memset(p.msg, ' ', PKT_MSG_LEN);
  for (uint8_t i = 0; i < PKT_MSG_LEN && i < text.length(); i++) p.msg[i] = text[i];
  p.crc8 = crc8((const uint8_t*)&p, sizeof(p) - 1);

  uint32_t t0 = millis();
  int16_t st = radio.transmit((uint8_t*)&p, sizeof(p));
  uint32_t dt = millis() - t0;

  Serial.print(F("[TX ] seq=")); Serial.print(seqNum);
  Serial.print(F(" ")); Serial.print(dt); Serial.print(F("ms \""));
  Serial.print(text); Serial.print(F("\" "));
  Serial.println(st == RADIOLIB_ERR_NONE ? F("OK") : F("FAILED"));
}

void handleLine(String s) {
  s.trim();
  if (!s.length()) return;

  if (s.equalsIgnoreCase("/tx off")) {
    txEnabled = false;
    radio.standby();
    Serial.println(F("[CMD] TX OFF"));
  } else if (s.equalsIgnoreCase("/tx on")) {
    txEnabled = true;
    applyRadioConfig();
    Serial.println(F("[CMD] TX ON"));
  } else if (s.equalsIgnoreCase("/freq main")) {
    useBackup = false;
    applyRadioConfig();
  } else if (s.equalsIgnoreCase("/freq back")) {
    useBackup = true;
    applyRadioConfig();
  } else if (s.equalsIgnoreCase("/status")) {
    Serial.print(F("[ST ] freq=")); Serial.print(useBackup ? FREQ_BACKUP : FREQ_MAIN, 2);
    Serial.print(F(" tx="));        Serial.print(txEnabled ? F("ON") : F("OFF"));
    Serial.print(F(" sent="));      Serial.println(seqNum);
  } else {
    pendingMsg = s.substring(0, PKT_MSG_LEN);
    sendPacket(pendingMsg);
  }
}

void feedChar(char c) {
  if (c == '\n' || c == '\r') {
    if (lineBuf.length()) { handleLine(lineBuf); lineBuf = ""; }
  } else {
    lineBuf += c;
    if (lineBuf.length() > 200) lineBuf = "";
  }
}

void loop() {
  while (Serial.available()) feedChar((char)Serial.read());
  if (millis() - lastBeacon >= BEACON_MS) {
    lastBeacon = millis();
    sendPacket(pendingMsg);
  }
}
```

ESP32 — Receiver, GCS-integrated (rebuilt from your proven pattern)
cpp

```cpp
#include <RadioLib.h>

#define TEAM_ID "025"

#define PIN_NSS    5
#define PIN_IRQ   26
#define PIN_RST   14
#define PIN_BUSY  27
#define PIN_SCK   18
#define PIN_MISO  19
#define PIN_MOSI  23

#define FREQ_MAIN      867.00f
#define LORA_SF        10
#define LORA_BW        125.0f
#define LORA_CR        5
#define LORA_SYNC      0x98
#define LORA_PREAMBLE  12
#define TCXO_VOLTAGE   1.6f     // proven working value on this hardware

#define PKT_MSG_LEN 10
#define PKT_MAGIC   0xA5

typedef struct __attribute__((packed)) {
  uint8_t  magic;
  uint8_t  teamId;
  uint32_t seq;
  uint32_t upMs;
  int8_t   txPower;
  uint8_t  paPath;
  uint8_t  flags;
  char     msg[PKT_MSG_LEN];
  uint8_t  crc8;
} RangePkt_t;
#define PKT_LEN ((uint8_t)sizeof(RangePkt_t))
static_assert(sizeof(RangePkt_t) == 24, "packet must be 24 bytes");

SPIClass loraSPI(HSPI);
LR1121 radio = new Module(PIN_NSS, PIN_IRQ, PIN_RST, PIN_BUSY, loraSPI);

static const uint32_t rfsw_pins[] = {
  RADIOLIB_LR11X0_DIO5, RADIOLIB_LR11X0_DIO6,
  RADIOLIB_NC, RADIOLIB_NC, RADIOLIB_NC
};
static const Module::RfSwitchMode_t rfsw_table[] = {
  { LR11x0::MODE_STBY,  { LOW,  LOW  } },
  { LR11x0::MODE_RX,    { HIGH, LOW  } },
  { LR11x0::MODE_TX,    { HIGH, HIGH } },
  { LR11x0::MODE_TX_HP, { LOW,  HIGH } },
  { LR11x0::MODE_TX_HF, { LOW,  LOW  } },
  { LR11x0::MODE_GNSS,  { LOW,  LOW  } },
  { LR11x0::MODE_WIFI,  { LOW,  LOW  } },
  END_OF_MODE_TABLE,
};

volatile bool pktFlag = false;
uint32_t packetCount = 0;
uint32_t startMs = 0;

IRAM_ATTR void onPacket() { pktFlag = true; }

static uint8_t crc8(const uint8_t* d, size_t n) {
  uint8_t c = 0;
  while (n--) {
    c ^= *d++;
    for (uint8_t i = 0; i < 8; i++)
      c = (c & 0x80) ? (uint8_t)((c << 1) ^ 0x07) : (uint8_t)(c << 1);
  }
  return c;
}

static bool rfCheck(const __FlashStringHelper* what, int16_t st) {
  if (st == RADIOLIB_ERR_NONE) return true;
  Serial.print(F("  !! ")); Serial.print(what);
  Serial.print(F(" -> code ")); Serial.println(st);
  return false;
}
#define RFC(n, c) rfCheck(F(n), (c))

void applyRxConfig() {
  RFC("setFrequency",       radio.setFrequency(FREQ_MAIN));
  RFC("setBandwidth",       radio.setBandwidth(LORA_BW));
  RFC("setSpreadingFactor", radio.setSpreadingFactor(LORA_SF));
  RFC("setCodingRate",      radio.setCodingRate(LORA_CR));
  RFC("setSyncWord",        radio.setSyncWord(LORA_SYNC));
  RFC("setPreambleLength",  radio.setPreambleLength(LORA_PREAMBLE));
  RFC("setCRC",             radio.setCRC(2));
  RFC("implicitHeader",     radio.implicitHeader(PKT_LEN));
  RFC("setRegulatorDCDC",   radio.setRegulatorDCDC());
  RFC("setRxBoostedGainMode", radio.setRxBoostedGainMode(true));
}

#define N_SCHEMA_FIELDS 79
#define IDX_TIME_S   0
#define IDX_PKT      1
#define IDX_RSSI     61
#define IDX_SNR      62
#define IDX_FREQ_MHZ 63

void sendGcsLine(float rssi, float snr, float freqMhz) {
  float t = (millis() - startMs) / 1000.0;
  Serial.print(TEAM_ID);
  Serial.print(",");
  for (int i = 0; i < N_SCHEMA_FIELDS; i++) {
    if (i == IDX_TIME_S)        Serial.print(t, 2);
    else if (i == IDX_PKT)      Serial.print(packetCount);
    else if (i == IDX_RSSI)     Serial.print(rssi, 1);
    else if (i == IDX_SNR)      Serial.print(snr, 1);
    else if (i == IDX_FREQ_MHZ) Serial.print(freqMhz, 2);
    if (i < N_SCHEMA_FIELDS - 1) Serial.print(",");
  }
  Serial.println();
}

void setup() {
  Serial.begin(115200);
  delay(600);
  Serial.println();
  Serial.println(F("======================================================"));
  Serial.println(F("  VRITRA TEAM 025 -- ESP32 LoRa RECEIVER (GCS)"));
  Serial.println(F("======================================================"));
  Serial.print(F("Pins: NSS=")); Serial.print(PIN_NSS);
  Serial.print(F(" IRQ=")); Serial.print(PIN_IRQ);
  Serial.print(F(" RST=")); Serial.print(PIN_RST);
  Serial.print(F(" BUSY=")); Serial.println(PIN_BUSY);

  loraSPI.begin(PIN_SCK, PIN_MISO, PIN_MOSI, PIN_NSS);
  radio.setRfSwitchTable(rfsw_pins, rfsw_table);   // before begin() -- matches proven file

  Serial.print(F("[RF ] begin() ... "));
  int16_t st = radio.begin(FREQ_MAIN, LORA_BW, LORA_SF, LORA_CR,
                           LORA_SYNC, 10, LORA_PREAMBLE, TCXO_VOLTAGE);
  if (st != RADIOLIB_ERR_NONE) {
    Serial.print(F("FAILED ")); Serial.println(st);
    while (1) delay(500);
  }
  Serial.println(F("OK"));
  radio.setRfSwitchTable(rfsw_pins, rfsw_table);   // after begin() too -- matches proven file

  LR11x0VersionInfo_t vi;
  if (radio.getVersionInfo(&vi) == RADIOLIB_ERR_NONE) {
    Serial.print(F("[RF ] HW 0x")); Serial.print(vi.hardware, HEX);
    Serial.print(F(" dev 0x"));     Serial.print(vi.device, HEX);
    Serial.print(F(" fw "));        Serial.print(vi.fwMajor);
    Serial.print(F("."));           Serial.println(vi.fwMinor);
  }

  applyRxConfig();
  radio.setPacketReceivedAction(onPacket);
  radio.startReceive();
  startMs = millis();

  Serial.println(F("Listening. Point your GCS at this COM port, 115200 baud."));
  Serial.println(F("======================================================"));
}

void loop() {
  if (!pktFlag) return;
  pktFlag = false;

  RangePkt_t p;
  int16_t st = radio.readData((uint8_t*)&p, PKT_LEN);
  if (st != RADIOLIB_ERR_NONE) { radio.startReceive(); return; }
  if (p.magic != PKT_MAGIC)    { radio.startReceive(); return; }
  if (crc8((const uint8_t*)&p, sizeof(p) - 1) != p.crc8) { radio.startReceive(); return; }

  packetCount++;
  float rssi = radio.getRSSI();
  float snr  = radio.getSNR();

  sendGcsLine(rssi, snr, FREQ_MAIN);

  char msgbuf[PKT_MSG_LEN + 1];
  memcpy(msgbuf, p.msg, PKT_MSG_LEN);
  msgbuf[PKT_MSG_LEN] = '\0';
  Serial.print(F("  ^ seq=")); Serial.print(p.seq);
  Serial.print(F(" RSSI=")); Serial.print(rssi, 1);
  Serial.print(F(" SNR="));  Serial.print(snr, 1);
  Serial.print(F(" msg=\"")); Serial.print(msgbuf);
  Serial.println(F("\""));

  radio.startReceive();
}
```

**CLAUDE** (2026-08-02T20:07:24)

This settles it — and it's genuinely good news for the parsing work, even though NavIC itself isn't showing satellites yet.

**`GIGSV sentences seen=10` proves the fix works.** The L89 is actively transmitting NavIC-tagged sentences every cycle, and your firmware is now correctly reading them (before this session's fix, TinyGPSPlus couldn't see `$GIGSV` at all — this was silently zero for a different reason). This is no longer a parsing bug.

**`in-view=0` consistently, while GPS+Galileo+BeiDou show 6-7 satellites at 42-46 dB-Hz, points to one specific remaining cause: antenna frequency response.**

NavIC doesn't share GPS's frequency. GPS/Galileo/BeiDou (the constellations giving you that excellent signal) all broadcast around **L1 (1575 MHz)**. NavIC broadcasts on **L5 (1176 MHz)** and S-band (2492 MHz) — a completely different part of the spectrum. Most low-cost combo GNSS patch antennas are tuned and matched for L1 only; their gain rolls off sharply outside that band, and L5 in particular is a common weak spot on cheap antennas that advertise "multi-GNSS" but were really only characterized at L1.

Your L1-band signal chain is proven excellent (42-46 dB-Hz is about as good as it gets). If the antenna's L5 response is poor, the receiver can still detect that IRNSS satellites exist (enough to emit `$GIGSV` sentences with the right talker ID) without actually locking onto any of them well enough to report them as "in view" with a usable signal.

This is a hardware characteristic of the antenna, not something firmware can fix. Two ways to actually confirm this diagnosis rather than just accept it:

1. **Check your antenna's datasheet/listing** for whether it explicitly claims L5 or NavIC support — many "GPS+GLONASS+Galileo+BeiDou" antennas simply don't mention NavIC at all, which is itself the answer.
2. **Try at a different time of day.** IRNSS's primary satellites are geostationary/geosynchronous, so if it's a geometry issue rather than antenna gain, satellite position won't change meaningfully — but if any of the inclined-orbit IRNSS satellites are what's needed and they're currently below your horizon, that would resolve at a different hour.

If the antenna genuinely lacks L5 gain, NavIC will stay at 0 regardless of software, and that's worth knowing now rather than continuing to chase it as a code problem. Given your GPS+Galileo+BeiDou performance is already strong (6-7 sats, HDOP 2.7-3.0), I'd treat NavIC as a stretch goal rather than block further GNSS work on it.

**CLAUDE** (2026-08-02T20:15:04)

That log is genuinely excellent — it climbed all the way to **12 satellites, HDOP 0.9**, held stable for the last several minutes, with SNR consistently 45-48 dB-Hz. This confirms the config is right. NavIC staying at 0 the whole time (consistent with the earlier antenna-L5 diagnosis) is expected, not a regression.

For "moving at high speed," there's one real config gap: **the receiver's internal dynamic platform model.** Most MTK-based GNSS chipsets (which the L89 is built on) default to a "Normal/Pedestrian" navigation filter that can reject or lag behind rapid altitude/velocity changes — exactly what a falling CanSat produces. There's a specific command for this that the log never sent. Adding it, plus fix-continuity monitoring so you can actually *prove* "maintains connection while moving" rather than assume it.

---
**CLAUDE** (2026-08-02T20:15:15)

Adding the two things this needs to actually prove flight-worthiness under motion: the aviation/high-dynamics navigation mode, and a fix-continuity monitor that catches dropouts instead of just showing you the good moments.

Clean compile.

## What this run actually proved, and what's new

Your log is the best result this GNSS has produced: **12 satellites, HDOP 0.9, SNR 45-48 dB-Hz, held for 8+ minutes.** That's a fully validated config — GPS+GLONASS+Galileo+BeiDou, SBAS, EASY, all confirmed working by direct measurement, not guesswork. NavIC staying at 0 the entire run is consistent, expected, and already explained (antenna L1-tuned, NavIC needs L5).

That result was measured **holding the antenna still.** Your actual requirement — "maintain connection while moving at high speed" — is a different property, and nothing in that log tested it. Two changes address that directly:

**1. `PMTK886,2` — Aviation dynamic mode.** This was the one real gap. MTK-based GNSS chipsets (the L89's core) default to a filter tuned for a person walking — it assumes position changes slowly and smoothly. A falling, spinning, chute-snapping CanSat violates that assumption constantly, and the default filter can lag or reject fixes during those events. Aviation mode raises the filter's tolerance for rapid velocity/acceleration changes specifically so it keeps tracking instead of freezing or discarding updates.

**2. Fix-continuity/dropout tracking — the actual motion test.** A bench log showing a smooth string of `[LIVE]` lines can't prove the receiver survives motion; it only proves it works sitting still. The sketch now times the gap between every consecutive valid fix and flags anything over 1.5 seconds as a dropout:

```
[DROPOUT] gap=2340 ms (expected ~1000 ms) — dropout #1
[CONT] fixes=142  dropouts=0  worstGap=1020 ms  -> clean — no gap exceeded threshold
```

**How to actually run the motion test:** flash it, wait for the fix to land (it'll print instructions), then physically walk with the antenna and swing it through different tilt angles while watching for `[DROPOUT]` lines. Compare the dropout count while moving against a stationary baseline — that comparison is the real evidence, not a single number in isolation. A CanSat's actual descent is far more violent than a hand-held walk, so this proves the config is measurably better under motion, not that it's flawless in flight — that's an honest limit of bench testing, not something more code can paper over.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - Quectel L89 GNSS module: `$PMTK` (MediaTek) vs `$PAIR` (Quectel-proprietary) command families — PMTK353 has no NavIC bit; NavIC is enabled/confirmed via `$PAIR066,1,0,1,0,0,1`.
   - `$PMTK886,2` — GNSS "Aviation" dynamic platform model (0=Normal/Pedestrian, 1=Fitness, 2=Aviation ≤40000m, 3=Balloon ≤80000m, 4=Stationary) — needed because MTK-chipset receivers default to a pedestrian-tuned Kalman filter that can lag/reject fixes during rapid velocity/altitude changes (freefall, chute deployment).
   - Fix-continuity/dropout tracking as a testable proxy for "maintains lock while moving," since a stationary bench test cannot itself prove this.
   - LR1121 (Core1121-XF) radio: HP vs LP PA path selection via `setOutputPower(N, true)`; TCXO_VOLTAGE must be empirically proven per-hardware-variant (1.6f proven correct on this hardware after an incorrect 0.0f inference from schematic reading); `setRfSwitchTable()` call-order relative to `radio.begin()`; barebone/config-free connection tests (reset pulse → watch BUSY pin → SPI.begin() → begin() with defaults → read chip version) as the fastest wiring-vs-firmware isolation tool; module-swap testing to isolate module vs. board faults.
   - I2C bus-sharing hazards: bus recovery (9 manual SCL pulses with SDA released) must run before every `Wire.begin()`, unconditionally, because all sensors share one physical bus and a wedge from an unrelated prior session/sketch can break the next boot's first transaction.
   - "Read-only diagnostics" principle: any diagnostic routine that performs a write (even seemingly innocuous, like DEVICE_RESET) can time out and leave the bus in a bad state that silently breaks a different, already-working device later in the same boot.
   - Register-write success ≠ correct device behavior: must validate configuration via an actual physically-plausible reading, not just absence of a bus error (BMP585 DSP_CONFIG incident).
   - Stillness-gated calibration: bias/reference calibration routines (gyro bias, accel reference rotation, barometer pad pressure) must check for actual measured stillness, not just run for a fixed sample count, or motion gets baked into the "baseline."
   - Two-ray ground reflection RF propagation model and its breakpoint-distance dependence on antenna height — used to explain the gap between a ~400m bench-range result and the theoretical/achievable ~2km link budget.
   - NavIC L5-band (1176 MHz) vs GPS/Galileo/BeiDou L1-band (1575 MHz) antenna gain mismatch as the leading (unconfirmed) explanation for persistent `NAVIC in-view=0`.

       3. Added global state and functions:
         ```cpp
         #define EXPECTED_FIX_INTERVAL_MS  1000UL
         #define DROPOUT_THRESHOLD_MS      ((EXPECTED_FIX_INTERVAL_MS * 3) / 2)
         static uint32_t lastFixMs      = 0;
         static bool     everHadFix     = false;
         static uint32_t dropoutCount   = 0;
         static uint32_t worstGapMs     = 0;
         static uint32_t fixUpdateCount = 0;

         static void trackFixContinuity() {
             uint32_t now = millis();
             fixUpdateCount++;
             if (everHadFix) {
                 uint32_t gap = now - lastFixMs;
                 if (gap > worstGapMs) worstGapMs = gap;
                 if (gap > DROPOUT_THRESHOLD_MS) {
                     dropoutCount++;
                     Serial.printf("[DROPOUT] gap=%lu ms (expected ~%lu ms) — dropout #%lu\n",
                                   (unsigned long)gap, (unsigned long)EXPECTED_FIX_INTERVAL_MS,
                                   (unsigned long)dropoutCount);
                 }
             }
             lastFixMs = now;
             everHadFix = true;
         }

         static void printContinuity() {
             Serial.printf("[CONT] fixes=%lu  dropouts=%lu  worstGap=%lu ms  -> %s\n",
                           (unsigned long)fixUpdateCount, (unsigned long)dropoutCount,
                           (unsigned long)worstGapMs,
                           dropoutCount == 0
                               ? "clean — no gap exceeded threshold"
                               : "gaps detected — move/reorient during test to compare");
         }
         ```

**CLAUDE** (2026-08-02T20:33:16)

This is the exact motion-continuity data the test was built to produce, and it validates the fix but also exposes something worth flagging honestly.

**Reading the log:**

- **Fix acquired at 53.8s**, satellites climbed 5→12, HDOP improved 4.6→2.4, bestSNR held 39-49 dB-Hz throughout — all consistent with the earlier findings, nothing new there.
- **Exactly 1 dropout, at the very start** (`gap=1652ms`, right after the first `[LIVE]` line at `fixes=2`). Every one of the next ~920 fix updates was clean — `dropouts=1` never incremented again through the entire ~15-minute log.
- **NavIC still `in-view=0`** the whole time — consistent with prior sessions, still unresolved (antenna L5 gain suspect, per [SENSOR_TESTING_FINDINGS.md](SENSOR_TESTING_FINDINGS.md) §9).

**The problem: this isn't actually a motion test.** Look at the lat/lon columns — they crawl by ~0.00001-0.00003° per fix for the first ~90s (normal walking-drift pattern), then **freeze bit-for-bit identical** (`31.247784, 75.703740`) from `20:26:09` clear through to the end of the log at `20:27:45`, nearly 100 seconds of zero position change down to the 5th decimal. That's not "held very still" — GPS noise alone would jitter the last digit even stationary. That pattern (identical lat/lon across dozens of consecutive fixes) reads as the antenna sat down and stopped moving, not as an active swing/tumble test. So the single dropout at the very beginning is most likely the acquisition-to-tracking transition settling, not a motion-induced loss of lock — and the "while moving" condition described in the header instructions was never actually exercised in this run.

**What this run actually proves:** stationary lock is rock solid post-acquisition (0 dropouts across ~860 fixes while still). It does **not** yet prove the CanSat requirement — connection maintained *while moving at high speed* — because the device wasn't moving for most of the log.

To get a real answer, rerun with continuous physical motion (walk/swing/rotate) for the full duration and watch whether `dropoutCount` climbs. Want me to also add an `[STATE]` tag to the sketch that logs a "possibly-stationary" flag (lat/lon delta below noise floor for N consecutive fixes) so future logs self-report whether the motion test was actually being performed, instead of relying on us eyeballing the coordinates after the fact?

**CLAUDE** (2026-08-02T20:34:00)

That confirms it — this was a pure stationary re-verification run, not the motion test. Good data to have on its own (0 dropouts across ~860 fixes, 15 minutes rock-solid while still, confirms the earlier fixes are stable), but it doesn't yet answer the actual requirement: does it hold lock while moving at high speed.

The single dropout at the start (`gap=1652ms`, right after acquisition) is expected — that's the receiver settling from "fix just acquired" into steady 1Hz tracking, not a fault.

**To actually test the motion requirement**, you need a run where you physically move/swing/rotate the antenna the whole time and watch `dropoutCount` in the `[CONT]` lines. Since walking wasn't practical for this test, a few options that better simulate CanSat dynamics anyway (walking pace doesn't represent descent/spin speeds well regardless):

1. **Swing test** — hold the module and swing your arm in circles/figure-8s continuously for 1-2 minutes, watching for `[DROPOUT]` lines.
2. **Spin test** — rotate the module in place (simulating tumble) rather than translating it — closer to what a falling/spinning CanSat actually does than walking.
3. **Car test** — mount it in a car and drive a short loop — gives you real velocity and vibration, the closest bench-accessible proxy to descent dynamics.

Any of those, even a couple minutes long, would give a real dropout-count comparison against this clean stationary baseline. Let me know which one you want to run and I'll watch for anything in the sketch that needs adjusting first.
